@@113264207 <p> Photo of damaged corals ( left ) and a diver ( right ) in Rozul Reef , located within the West Philippine Sea <p> Released / WESCOM <p> Leading up to September as the Maritime and Archipelagic Nation Month ( MANA Mo ) pursuant to Proclamation 316 , series of 2017 , and World Maritime Day on the 26th , the immense value of the manifold marine biodiversity in our territorial sea of 679,800 square kilometers comes to the fore . <p> With the Philippines , particularly the Verde Island Passage Marine Corridor being referred to as the center of the center of marine shore fish biodiversity , it is the 11th largest producer of fish and fish products globally . Marine resources are also key to the country 's food security : if kanin ( rice ) is life , then fish and fish products certainly make Filipinos ' lives sweeter and more robust for being the highest source of animal protein meat . <p> Moreover , according to the DOST , the Philippines ' coral reef area which spans 26,000 square kilometers -- the second largest in Southeast Asia -- is home to over 500 coral @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ coastal and marine ecosystems are vital to migratory species that are not limited to sea life such as whales , sharks , turtles , and dolphins but also include waterbirds that rely on the ecological connectivity of these habitats for breeding and nesting , as they traverse across the Russian Far East and Alaska , East Asia and Southeast Asia , to Australia and New Zealand . <p> The Stratbase ADR Institute hosted a forum titled " Coral Reefs &amp; Currents : Prioritizing Marine Conservation in the West Philippine Sea " on Aug. 27 , 2024 , at the Asian Institute of Management . <p> Ms. Lea Avilla of the Biodiversity Management Bureau ( BMB ) outlined government strategies for marine resource protection , highlighting the Coastal and Marine Ecosystems Management Program and noting that 1.42% of the Philippines ' sea area is now designated as Marine Protected Areas ( MPAs ) . <p> Indeed , addressing the triple planetary crisis of biodiversity loss , climate change , and pollution , and an array of other cross-cutting issues require synergies among policies , as well as initiatives among the public @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ local communities , partners , and the international community . <p> Included in these collective efforts is the Philippines ' commitments under multilateral environmental agreements ( MEAs ) , such as the Convention on Biological Diversity ( CBD ) , the UN Framework Convention on Climate Change ( UNFCCC ) , the Convention on Migratory Species ( CMS ) , the Convention on the International Trade in Endangered Species of Wild Flora and Fauna ( CITES ) , and the Convention on Wetlands of International Importance . These MEAs have distinct but intertwined objectives , just like the ecosystems that they seek to protect . <p> The Kunming-Montreal Global Biodiversity Framework , or the Biodiversity Plan is the most recent expression of the synergies that are required , if our shared vision to live in harmony with nature by 2050 is to come into fruition , through the achievement of its 4 long-term Goals , and the accomplishment of its 23 Targets by 2030 . <p> The first three goals embody the objectives of the CBD -- the conservation and sustainable use of biodiversity , and the fair and equitable @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ while fourth seeks adequate means of implementation of the Biodiversity Plan . <p> Integral to the latter is the equitable access by all Parties to the CBD , especially by developing States such as the Philippines , of financial resources , capacity-building , technical and scientific cooperation , and access to and transfer of technology . <p> The realization of these Goals is contingent on progress in reaching the Targets . Of particular importance are Target 3 or the 30x30 Target that seeks the effective conservation and management of at least 30% of land , waters and seas ; Target 8 on minimizing the impacts of climate change and ocean acidification through nature-based solutions and ecosystem-based approaches ; and Target 7 on reducing pollution from all sources . Moreover , the Biodiversity Plan engenders policymaking that is backed by the best available science , an inclusive and rights-based implementation , and the mainstreaming of biodiversity into various sectors . <p> The Philippines is taking strides toward the right direction with the passage of the Philippine Ecosystem and Natural Capital Accounting System ( PENCAS ) Act , which institutionalizes the valuation @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ accounting framework , while recognizing the intrinsic value of nature . <p> Additionally , the Securities and Exchange Commission has issued guidelines on blue bonds for financing eligible projects relating to coastal , marine , and water ecosystems and resources . The Philippines has also signed -- but has yet to ratify -- the Agreement under the United Nations Convention on the Law of the Sea on the Conservation and Sustainable Use of Marine Biological Diversity of Areas beyond National Jurisdiction ( BBNJ Agreement ) . <p> Despite the rising geopolitical tensions at the West Philippine Sea , perhaps various avenues remain open for amity and cooperation -- if only to reduce the degradation of the marine environment and the risk of biodiversity depletion -- by buoying up synergies among domestic policies and efforts , MEAs , and the impetus of the Biodiversity Plan . @@113284417 <h> Hindy Weber cultivates beauty from the ground up <h> From high fashion to organic farming and now wholly natural skincare , Hindy Weber expands environmental consciousness through projects rooted in nature and mindfulness <p> " Farming " and " fashion " are n't words you 'd typically pair together . Yet for Hindy Weber , the two go hand-in-hand , tied closely to the way of life she upholds . <p> Having been a longtime fixture in the fashion cosmos , Weber embraces her years of experience with grace and wisdom . As she grounds herself -- quite literally -- in the earth , she channels her energies into sustainable farming , fashion , and her latest venture , natural skincare . <p> " I do n't see any difference between my farming work , my fashion work , and my skincare that I do , " she explains . " <p> While forest bathing offers both mental and physical rejuvenation , @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ similar holistic healing experience , echoing the life-giving vibrations of her happy place , Holy Carabao Farm . As she walks around the farm donning her all-natural slow fashion designs , Weber shares how her projects cultivate more than just crops but also a philosophy that supports living in tune with the earth . <h> From fashion to farming <p> " My first foray into fashion was as a model , " Weber recalls . " I think I was 13 or 14 years old when I started modeling . And I did n't really feel comfortable . I was so shy . " But this led her to seek out her true calling -- finding joy in fashion design . <p> After finishing a communication arts degree at Ateneo de Manila University , Weber pursued a postgraduate degree at New York 's Fashion Institute of Technology . <p> For years , Weber became a fixture in Manila 's retail industry . <p> But the rigid structure and frenetic pace eventually took its toll . " Back in the 2000s when I was a young designer , we had a @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ to follow , and marketing strategies and OTBs , or open to buys . It was very driven by global market trends . Even the colors . " <p> " In a sense it helped me because it was very disciplined , " clarifies Weber . " But after 10 years , I got burnt out , churning out design after design . Then it kind of lost the spirit for me . " <p> At this time , Weber took a sabbatical and discovered her passion for produce , long before the rise of urban farming . <p> " I moved to Laguna and I started growing food in the backyard . I just hired one gardener . He was n't really a farmer . But we learned together , and we started cultivating more of the land around our house , " Weber shares . <p> This humble beginning evolved into Holy Carabao Farm , a purely educational farm that teaches children and families about sustainable agriculture , and growing produce for the farm 's next-door school community , the Acacia Waldorf School in Laguna . <p> While @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ to slow and sustainable design practices , crafting clothes from organic materials only . <h> The Holy Carabao Farm <p> Weber lights up whenever she talks about her kids and the work she does at the farm . Located right next to the Acacia Waldorf School , there are roaming ducks , a rabbit and guinea pig pen , and best of all , a carabao bathing in the mud , which takes visitors on the occasional joyride . <p> Weber references the 2020 documentary " Kiss the Ground , " narrated by Woody Harrelson , which highlights soil regeneration as a key solution to climate change . The farm and adjacent school serve as the Philippine affiliate for this initiative , aligning the documentary 's principles into the grade school curriculum . They have also tailored the resource to our local context , from weather conditions to the cultural nuances in communities . <p> Education seems to be a priority in Weber 's work , both through her farm and the school , where she heads the farming committee . " It 's a very active community , " @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ of parent who just wants to drop off their kids . They 're always going to have you volunteer for something , and they 'll find what you like as a parent . " <p> " As a young girl , I was always into the environment . I was part of Greenpeace and a member of the World Wildlife Fund . I wanted to be a zoologist ... I did n't become a scientist and I did n't become an environmentalist because my path went into creative fashion ... but when we started the farm , that was a full circle moment . " <h> Au naturel skincare <p> Weber 's regenerative approach to beauty shows in her daily routine , too , which she clarifies as personal and unique to her needs . " I 'm 51 now , and I have my own bodily hormonal changes that I have to deal with ... I love the sun , and I get a lot of my healing from the full light spectrum from the sun . " <p> In 2021 , in between COVID-19 lockdowns , Weber launched her own skincare line . The products were developed in collaboration with natural formulator Ana Gutierrez of Body Food . " Everything that I use on my face , I 've been researching for 20+ years ... Just like my food , I 'm very careful about what I put on my skin . @@113284459 <h> Frogs saved from flesh-eating fungus with the help of a sauna <p> This picture taken on August 13 , 2024 , shows a biologist holding green and golden bell frogs , an endangered species , at the research center of Macquarie University in Sydney . -- Hundreds of endangered Green and Golden Bell frogs huddle inside a sauna , protected from Sydney 's winter chill . The sauna -- a small greenhouse containing black-painted bricks -- may offer a pleasant warmth , but it is also protecting the frogs from the deadly chytrid fungus that would otherwise drive them to extinction . ( Photo by Saeed KHAN / AGENCE FRANCE-PRESSE ) <p> The sauna -- a small greenhouse containing black-painted bricks warmed by the sun -- may be pleasant , but it also protects the frogs from a deadly chytrid fungus that would otherwise drive them to extinction . <p> Macquarie University biologist Anthony Waddle holds one frog -- no bigger than a credit card -- in his hand as its green and gold colors become more vibrant in the heat . <p> It is a water-borne disease that burrows into the frogs ' skin , attacking their @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ globally , the disease has caused the decline of 500 amphibian species and driven 90 to extinction -- six in Australia . <p> Article continues after this advertisement <p> " Nothing has ever caused this much devastation , " he told Agence France-Presse . " In Australia , we have frogs that only live in glass boxes now . This is a huge , ongoing problem . " <p> Article continues after this advertisement <p> But Waddle 's dollhouse-sized saunas could change that . <p> Article continues after this advertisement <p> In their warm interiors , the deadly chytrid fungus can not grow on the jumping amphibian , allowing them to fight off the infection and survive . <h> Frogs extinction : ' No one solution ' <p> Frogs play a vital role in the environment and are known as bioindicators , which are used to assess the health of ecosystems . <p> Article continues after this advertisement <p> Without the amphibians , entire ecosystems can collapse . <p> Globally , 41 percent of frog species are threatened with extinction , making them one of the most vulnerable invertebrate groups @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ of Nature found . <p> Some of the biggest drivers include loss of habitat , climate change , and the chytrid disease . <p> These drivers are difficult to tackle , but in the absence of a cure , Waddle hopes his frog saunas can help limit the losses . <p> " This might be the first evidence that we could cheaply and feasibly reduce that nasty yearly die-off of frogs , " Waddle said . <p> " For Green and Golden Bell frogs , that could mean the difference between a population going or persisting . " <p> He said the saunas show that creative solutions ranging from the complex -- such as identifying genes that could make individuals resistant to chytrid -- to more simple are needed if frog populations are to survive . <p> " Not one solution is going to work for everything . Frogs are so different , " Waddle added . <p> But the beauty of Waddle 's saunas is that they cost AUD$70 ( $US50 ) to assemble , and he has helped dozens of citizen scientists build their own backyard versions . @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ frog species but also saved some from the deadly fungus . <p> Jodi Rowley , an amphibian biologist from the Australian Museum , said the saunas showed how creative solutions could have real-world benefits . <p> " It 's easy to feel helpless in the face of biodiversity declines , but this study gives us a tangible way we may be able to help frogs battling a devastating fungal disease , " she told Agence France-Presse.
